## River Ma'at

The River Ma'at was a wide, slow-moving body of water that twisted through much of the Outlands.

Geography
The source of this river was a large lake near the center of the Spire, particularly a divine realm half of it known as Semuanya's Bog. It regularly overflowed at its lower banks, carving a path through the rolling hills of the plane.

The River Ma'at mostly flowed along the seventh ring (or layer) of the Outlands, occasionally crossing through other layers. It passed by the gate-towns of Torch and Ribcage, the divine realm of Thoth's Estate, and the gate-towns of Automata, Fortitude, and Excelsior, until finally terminating at a shining salt flat. The distance between the River Ma'at and Torch was roughly a journey of three to eighteen days.

Throughout the area of Thoth's Estate, the River Ma'at was bordered on either side by deserts and swamps. There were also a number of villages and towns that dotted its banks in this realm, inhabited largely by the petitioners of Thoth.

Flora & Fauna
It was quite common to come across crocodiles and crocodile-like creatures living within the River Ma'at.

History
During the reign of King Azoun Obarskyr IV, a half-elven paladin of Oghma from Cormyr was dispatched on a mission to explore the planes of existence beyond Toril. The paladin's name was Ambran the Seeker, and excerpts from his journal still survived to the Present Age. In volume 7, Ambran described how he hired a bariaur guide named Glin to take him from Sigil to the Outlands. Then Volume 9 told of his journey from Ribcage to Automata. Once there, he was able to witness the Modron Parade before continuing on to the River Ma'at, accompanied by both Glin and Rialiva, a member of the Doomguard.

## Ring of crimson

Rings of Crimson were common minor magic items found across the Sword Coast in the late 14 century DR.

Powers
These rings were imbued with simple enchantments that made them glow with soft red light, illuminating the area of  around them.

History
By 1372 DR, these common pieces of enchanted jewelry were found across many pockets, fingers, and merchant stalls across the Sword Coast, and the Moonsea, and in cites such as Neverwinter and Daggerford. Especially popular among adventurers, freeing their hand for weapons and tools, instead of holding lanterns and torches. One of these rings was found in an ancient crypt in the Brush of Barbs of Delimbiyr Vale.

## Rigus

Rigus was a large gate-town leading to Acheron from the Outlands.

Description
Rigus was more of a military fortress than a town. It was built on a massive hill, divided into seven rings that rose to a pinnacle. Massive octagonal iron battlements and walls separated each ring, as well as the gate-town itself. The uppermost level was called the Crown. Entrance into Rigus was through several gates in the lowest, and largest, ring. The exterior of the lower wall was treated with a mild poison to prevent rust monsters from feeding on it.

Gates
Deep below the fortress, the gate to Acheron was located in a large chamber. The main entrance was at the bottom of a long staircase that connected the Crown to the gate. The gate itself was within an archway made from the bones of various bariaur. The gate was green-yellow in color with a stripe of black down the middle, somewhat resembling the eye of a cat. As such, it was also known as the Lion's Gate.

Government
The gate-town was ruled by groups called military orders. There were roughly one hundred such groups of various size and strength. Each military order was led by a general, and the generals ruled the city. However, the true source of power in Rigus was found in the Crown. Here, the spirits of former generals remained in a state of half-life, somewhat similar to that of liches. They were the true rulers of Rigus and they gave their orders and commands to the generals of the military order, who obeyed without question.

Inhabitants
The inhabitants of Rigus were primarily humans and tieflings. Every inhabitant was given a military rank and was expected to respect the military hierarchy and obey anyone with a higher rank without hesitation. When born, an inhabitant was given the rank of citizen. This rank was also given to foreigners. Promotion was given by the Great Generals. Ranks followed a typical military structure, one could be promoted from private, sergeant, lieutenant, captain, and eventually general. Anyone who broke the law, or those taken prisoner during raids, were given the rank of slave-soldier. Disobedience was considered a high crime on par with treason and was punishable by death.

Visitors were not given a warm welcome. Most who came to Rigus were encouraged to conduct whatever business brought them to the gate-town as quickly as possible. They were given a special plaque made from slate, which indicated they were unfamiliar with the hierarchy and laws of Rigus. This plaque meant that a visitor was not expected to have the same unquestionable obedience as the locals, but they were expected to show respect and obey as much as possible. Disrespectful visitors often had their plaques destroyed, leaving them vulnerable to the strict laws of Rigus, and were usually then taken as a slave-soldier.

## Ring of affliction

A ring of affliction was a cursed magic item that appeared to be a ring of resistance.

Powers
The ring actually functioned just like a ring of resistance until the caster attempted to use the ring's magic to resist an opposing school of magic that it was attuned to, at which point the wearer would find it more difficult to resist the effects of whatever triggered the magic. Once activated in this manner, the curse of the ring prevented it from providing the benefits of a ring of resistance and could only be removed from the finger by a remove curse spell.

Notable Owners
Nhar-del, a Zhentarim wizard inhabiting the Dalelands, wore a ring of affliction attuned to necromancy.

## Ring of teleportation

A ring of teleportation was a magic item that allowed the wearer to teleport.

Description
These rings were fitted with a deep blue gem worth 500 gp.

Powers
The user separated the gem from the ring and placed it in a location of their choosing, allowing them to teleport without error to that location by expending one of the ring's charges. If the location of the gem was unsafe in any way then the ring expended four charges but nothing happened. The gem could be moved to a new location at any time.

These rings had 3–30 charges.

Notable Owners
 Nisstyre, captain of the Dragon's Hoard mercenary company of Ched Nasad.
 Random Riellor, the world-traveling necromancer from Halruaa, wore a ring of teleportation.

Rift dragons were so feared by fishermen of the Sea of Moving Ice, they would quickly cut their line whenever they felt they might have hooked one.

## Rinyon Merrymar

Rinyon Merrymar was a strongheart halfling shopkeeper in the village of Shining Falls in the Delimbiyr Vale in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Relations
She was the wife of Blaz Merrymar. Together, they had two daughters and four sons, as well a number of nephews.

Activities
Rinyon handled the day-to-day running of Merrymar's Merchandise, the only general store in Shining Falls.

Personality
She was known to be confident and strong-willed, and felt her husband's get-rich-quick schemes, such as discovering the dwarven complex behind the Shining Falls waterfall, to be foolish.

History
In the summer of, goods meant for Merrymar's Merchandise were raided on the road by bandits, thought to be orcs of the High Forest. Tragically, Blaz and Rinyon lost not only merchandise but also all four of their sons and three of their nephews, leaving the extended family decimated and the store understaffed. Rinyon and her two daughters were left to run it alone while Blaz went on the road as a merchant.

## Ring of power

Rings of power were pieces of protective enchanted jewelry that gained popularity in the late 14 century DR across the Sword Coast North.

Powers
These enchanted ring were created of two types: regular rings of power and lesser rings of power. The lesser variation simply granted the wearer magical defenses against cold, electricity, and fire harm as well as the powers of regeneration. Regular rings of power granted significantly more potent magical defenses, regeneration, and gave the wearer the boon of freedom.

History
Originally, rings of power created on the behest of Mynseril Lostbone a powerful Malarite priest. He cherished blood-soaked brawls and wanted to find a way to prolong the ferocious pleasure. Mynseril started studying trolls and their uncanny regenerative powers, eventually coming up with the idea to outfit the most savage warriors with regenerative magics. Mynseril Lostbone commissioned the creation of rings of power and routinely gifted them to the strongest warriors from opposing factions who were to meet on the battlefield against each other.

In 1372 DR, regular and lesser rings of power were available for purchase in the Temple of Tyr of Port Llast, from cleric named Neurik and later in Beorunna's Well. The same year, Oleff Uskar of the Hall of Justice in Neverwinter offered these rings to his allies in the war with Luskan.

Circa 1372 DR, several powerful magical items were brought to Toril by a planeswalking wizard Vejoni H. Wyzz who pitched his extraplanar tent just outside the town of Liam's Hold. Lesser rings of power could be found in his collection.

## Ring of clear thought

Rings of clear thought were enchanted rings that enhanced the wearer's mental prowess.

Powers
These rings were ensorcelled to increase the wearer's intelligence and created with a wide variety of enchantment strengths.

History
Rings of clear thought came into prominence after Resino from the town of Beregost became tormented by ghostly voices inside his mind. Eventually, the suffering became too much to handle, and the man begged clerics of the local church to end his suffering even if it were through death. The priests could not detect the cause of torment nor any demonic afflictions placed on the suffering man. The only remedy they could think of was gifting the man one of the rings of clear thought, hoping that the enchantment would clear and shield Resino's mind. Unfortunately, the ring's magic was useless against the affliction, and Resino took his own life sometime before 1372 DR.

By the late 14 century DR many copies and variants of rings of clear thought could be found throughout the Sword Coast.

## River Lench

The River Lench, sometimes considered an eastern twin of the Sidewinder River and called by the same name, was a seldom-travelled river that served as the clearest boundary between Damara and Impiltur.

Description
The Lench flowed gently, at least relative to other rivers in the region. As nothing was imported from upstream in the Dunwood, the river saw little traffic north of Lenchford. However, it served the vital strategic purpose of being a clear barrier separating Damara and Impiltur, acting as a highly defensible and obvious border between them.

Geography
The Lench began in the southern peaks of the Giantspire Mountains and made its way southwest through the Rawlinswood (later called the Dunwood). It then exited the forest to flow between Impultur on the southern bank and the Damaran Barony of Polten on the northern bank. It ran through the only major crossing point at Lenchford before joining the Great Imphras River just north of Bluefang Water.

History
During the Vaasan War in the mid–14 century DR, Impiltur became increasingly concerned with the evil aspirations of Zhengyi the Witch-King, although not enough to aid their northern neighbors in the fight against him. With Damara's capitulation to the Witch-King in the, Impiltur mustered their armies and readied themselves for a war, but remained confident that the forces of Vaasa would not be able to invade across the Lench or the Sidewinder.

## Riyataivin

Riyataivin was a merchant and investor in caravan trades in the city of Waterdeep in the 14 century DR.

Activities
Riyataivin had no set house in Waterdeep, but rather spent time in various different rooming houses and inns in the Southern Ward. In terms of business, he made consistent profit margins by dealing in the buying and selling of cargo, draft animals, wagons, and warehouse space.

Relationships
Riyataivin was generally well respected among his colleagues. He had no particularly special knowledge or connection or the Lords of Waterdeep.

In 1357 DR,Page 14 of Waterdeep and the North states that the news provided in the Forgotten Realms Campaign Set, "...is current [to this book] as of early Mirtul, in the Year of the Prince. the Shadow Thieves of Athkatla sent an assassin to kill Riyataivin, having mistaken his penchant for acting mysterious as evidence of him being a Lord of Waterdeep. Armed with poisoned daggers, the assassin attacked an adventurer by mistake who closely resembled the merchant.

## Riautar's Weaponry

Riautar's Weaponry was a weapons shop in the city of Waterdeep that was owned by Zarondar Riautar in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Location
This shop was located along the High Road, just east of the Street of the Tusks, in the Trades Ward of Waterdeep.

Structure
Riautar's Weaponry stood two stories tall. On the roof of the building was a petrified harpy, with wings spread out and body crouched.

Services
This shop sold a variety of mundane and magical weapons, being very crowded with stock, most of it secondhand. They were particularly noteworthy for their finely crafted arrows and longbows, which were made on site by the proprietor.

Inhabitants
In addition to the proprietor Zarondar, Riautar's Weaponry employed the fletcher Hragnor Splitstone.

History
The petrified harpy on the building's roof dated back to the War of Five Wizards, wherein a wizard teleported three harpies into the city from a distant place and soon after another of the five wizards petrified them. Two of the harpies plummeted to the ground, shattering and crushing their summoner, while the third survived and became the statue atop Riataur's Weaponry.

Reputation
Riautar's Weaponry was among the more notable and recognizable shops in the Trades Ward. It was widely considered to be one of the best sources for arrows, bowstrings, and longbows in Waterdeep, while the shop advertised itself as being one of the best sources in all of the North. Their arrows and longbows were both admired and coveted by any archer who saw them.

Rumors & Legends
Urban legends claimed that some mage within Waterdeep would occasionally free the harpy from her petrified state, sending her out to kill for him, then force her back into her customary stone state. Over the years no evidence towards this rumor ever surfaced.

## Riverside Teahouse

The Riverside Teahouse, also known as the Gnarled Teahouse, was the home of the green hag Auntie Ethel for the years leading up to 15 century DR. While it appeared as a meager house situated within the riverside swamp in the Heartlands, it was masked an old and ancient place, rich with magic originating from other planes of existence.

Location
The shack was located on a raised patch of dirt amid the murky waters of the Putrid Bog along the northern bank of the River Chionthar on the Sword Coast.

Structure

Powerful illusion magic permeated the swamp and the teahouse itself, making it appear as a cozy little cottage built within a sunny fen. Its thatched room was covered in moss for insulation, while its façade appeared decorated with beauteous ivy and wildflowers that provided vibrant colors.

In truth, the dilapidated shack was overgrown with twisting vines, with massive rotting tree boughs that threatened to collapse its decrepit roof. A collection of bones from various creatures decorated the entrance to the hag's abode, some that were larger that a full-grown man.

Interior

Teahouse
The illusory form of the teahouse appeared as a dusty but charming apothecary's home, filled with vibrant plans and flowers, illuminated by beams of sunshine shining through small holes between its thatched roof. A tall stone hearth provided warmth and light for Ethel as she formulated her alchemical concoctions.

The true interior of Ethel's home mirrored the same disparity of its exterior appearance. Strangling vines snaked down its poorly-lit walls and shadows twisted across the floor. While it offered light, the fire in the shack's hearth was also an illusion, formed to conceal a passageway that led down into the hag's underground domain.

Entrance Gallery

Heading down the tunnel behind the false fireplace, a rickety wooden staircase led into to a single cavernous chamber – the entrance gallery of Ethel's domain. The overgrow root system of the swamp's massive trees ran over, across, and through the walls within, occasionally suspending within them purulent bulbs of explosive gases. Ethel's bubbling cauldron sat within the room's center, surrounded by the poor souls that were victimized by the horrific "help" she offered them.

Among the poor wretches that suffered from Ethel's involvement in their lives included Efrin Hammerborn, the diseased dwarven man who was spared death by means of petrification, the elf Lorin who sought knowledge of the future and was rewarded with visions of terrible mind flayer invaders, and the beheaded Magron who sought great intellect before betraying the hag. The spirit one of one particularly vain man remained trapped within a mirror, while the eye of a blinded elf woman remained pierced atop a spike next to her corpse.

Perhaps most notable among Ethel's victims was the enormous, tormented face that appeared within the tangled roots that barred passage to the depths of her lair. This door of tangled wood could share images and feelings with some empathic link to those that entered the gallery but could not otherwise communicate. While it was given the purpose of protecting her inner sanctum – ironic given that it was once a thief that dared to steal from the powerful hag – its form was just a mere illusion, similar to that which obscured Ethel's home and true physical form.

Overgrown Tunnel

A narrow passageway opened up beyond the illusory door, opening into a wider cavern reminiscent of the passage beneath the teahouse. Raised stone platforms dotted the middle of the cave, some of which were connected with the same purulent bulbs dangling from some of the more massive underground roots. This chamber was the domain of Ethel's masked minions, the unfortunate individuals that were compelled by her magic to sacrifice their lives to defend her dark and secretive magics. At the rear of the cave was a waterfall that gave way to the furthest depths of Ethel's refuge.

A leap through the waterfall led to a large stone outcropping that overlooked the enormity of the final cave of Ethel's domain. Dwarfing the previous chambers with its massive size, this immense opening was easily larger than all the others before it combined. Stone pillars and rocky tors were connected by twisted roots and wooden planks set precariously along their edge. Clouds of toxic, flammable gas lingered around rocky walkways, providing true peril for anyone that dared hazard across.

Hidden on a small cliff behind a cluster of vines was a secret room that house one of Ethel's numerous faerie ring, a circle of mushrooms that surrounded a swirling vortex that led to an area of the Underdark near the Arcane Tower.

At the heart of this cavern was the entryway to Ethel's true safe haven. Just beyond the meager wooden door that led within was a circular cage made of wood and bone. It was suspended from above by narrow roots and vines that were easily severed or set aflame.

Ancient Abode

Ethel's abode was situated in one of the deepest chambers within the cave network. Dead trees, winding vines and twisting roots served as its decorations while a single column of light offered some form of natural illumination. The focal point of the room was a large stone altar, stained with blood and littered with humanoid bones.

Ethel's acrid laboratory was situated along the chamber's eastern side, housing her collection of terrible potions, labeled with names like Missing Pets, Butterflies in the Stomach, and Insanity's Kiss.

At the rear of the abode was a small faerie ring, within which was set an exit  portal that could be used to escape the dreadful place.

Atmosphere
While the illusory form of the teahouse was quite inviting — to lull Ethel's petitioners into a false sense of comfort as they made requests of her — its true appearance reflected that of the hag's character, dark and malevolent. As troubling as the aboveground shack was, the further one ventured into the caves beneath the more horrid and disturbing the environment became.

Activities
The teahouse was the venue at which Ethel received individuals that came to her from all across the Realms. While these petitioners requested aid from the hag for the various tragedies or maladies that afflicted their lives, she offered them twisted deals that seldom if ever ended benefited their lives.

Defenses
The riverside teahouse was protected by the powerful illusion magic that masqueraded its true appearance along with that of the surrounding wetlands. Ethel kept a small army of red caps disguised as sheep that frolicked around the area.

In truth, Ethel needed few defenses to protect her home. She was a formidable spellcaster that was more that capable of dealing with possible intruders and potentially transforming them into magical tree-root doors.

History
At some point, a trio of holy warriors attempted to storm the depths of Ethel's lair, making it as far as the Overgrown Tunnel. While their ultimate fate was unknown they were last heard screaming in terror as they passed beyond the Entrance Gallery.

Some time in the, the young woman Mayrina came to stay at the riverside teahouse after making a deal with Ethel. Mayrina offered up her unborn child in exchange for the return of her husband Connor. Connor's body was kept in a casket near the shack, protected with some manner of arcane barrier.

At the beginning of the street, on either side of the River Gate, it was the terminus to Wall Street and Wall Way, two roads that ran along the Trollwall. ## Riturba

House Riturba was a noble family of Sumbria, one of the city-states of the Blade Kingdoms.

History
Sometime before 1217 DR House Riturba fell on hard times, and they loaned a significant sum of ducats from Gilberto Ilégo, a Blade Captain from a major Blade House of Sumbria. Ilégo was a cold, power-hungry, and cruel man. The Blade Captain foreclosed on House Riturba's loan, s deed that caused outrage. House Riturba accused Gilberto Ilégo of being a contemptible cheat, in response, Ilégo challenged the young noble Riturba accuser to a duel.

The duel took place during the Blade Festival that followed one of the Blade Kingdoms' annual "white wars." The Riturba youth was no match for a master duelist. The youth fenced using a rapier and a buckler, while Gilberto Ilégo used a rapier and a short swordbreaker. The House Riturba nobleman was slain in the duel, surrounded by witnesses and overseen by a gray-bearded umpire.

## River Mulsantir

The River Mulsantir was a prominent river found between Rashemen and Thay in the Unapproachable East.

Geography
The northern section of the River Mulsantir flowed north into Lake Ashane
while the southern section flowed west from the Sunrise Mountains to form Lake Mulsantir in southern Rashemen. The river formed part of the border between Rashemen and Thay as it passed through the Gorge of Gauros.

Description
The neck of the river found between Lake Ashane and Lake Mulsantir was considerably wider when compared to the section of the river that flowed south from Lake Mulsantir. The river would freeze during winter so that caravans could pass safely, but the melt in early spring left it unpassable for several tendays until the flow subsided.

Notable Locations
Mulsantir was located along the river's north shore where it flowed out of Lake Ashane and into Mulsantir Lake.

## Ritual of the Dark Flames

The Ritual of the Dark Flames, or Ritual of Dark Flames, was a supernatural ritual associated with the Cyricist faith, developed and performed during the late 14 century DR.

Activities
While the exact specifications of the ritual were undisclosed, it required the use of a black onyx gem worth at least 100 gp.

Outcome
The Ritual of the Dark Flames allowed a loyal devotee of Cyric to conjure and wield black flames born from negative energy itself. The use of this supernatural power was etremely taxing on the mind and will of its wielder.

It was regularly performed upon members of the Company of the Ebon Spur, to bolster their prowess on the battlefield.

History
The ritual was developed some time during or after the, when Cyric drove himself mad by reading from the Cyrinishad. It was still being performed regularly as of the .

## Riliton Mandleweave

Riliton Mandleweave was a powerful invoker who used her arcane prowess to the defend of her home of Hlondeth in 146 DR.

History
Riliton Mandleweave learned her impressive skills to weave spells from a renowned wizard Charas of Winterwood. She earned her place by defending the city of Hlondeth in the siege of . The siege was the second attempt to claim the city by the ambitious and militaristic leader of Turmish, Lord Dempster Turmish. He did not plan the siege accordingly and was stumped by Hlondeth's high walls, and his forces were held at bay by Riliton Mandleweave's barrage of spells. As she handled the enemy's forces upfront, the Company of the Howling Harpoon flanked the army and destroyed Dempster Turmish's siege machinery, food, and other supplies. The siege was a failure, and Turmite armies were turned around.

## River Isis

The River Isis was a waterway found in the realm of Heliopolis, in both the Great Wheel and World Tree models of the multiverse, that was named for the Mulhorandi goddess Isis.

Description
The waters of the River Isis were cool and clear. Uniquely, in the Great Wheel cosmology they were said to be tinged with a magic that prevented them from becoming polluted no matter what was put into them. The water was also said to act like holy water, but only while it was within Isis's city of Gizekhtet. If it was taken beyond the city, it would be normal water.

Geography
In the Great Wheel cosmology, the River Isis was said to run solely through the city of Gizekhtet, in the divine realm of the same name. In the World Tree model, the river was said to instead flow all throughout the desert of Heliopolis, irrigating the land and providing a source for all life in the plane. Along its banks stood the city of Thebestys and it passed through the city of Gizekhtet.

 In the bazaars of Zakhara, riding boots could generally be bought for around five to two gold pieces, depending upon how well one haggled.

## Ring-nose

The Ring-nose tribe was a clan of orcs living in Vaasa as of the mid-to-late 14 century DR. They were named after their distinctive iron nose rings.

Tactics
The Ring-nose planned dangerous ambushes. They would pick an ambush site along a road, dig a series of  deep pits around the edges, and then lie in wait on either side, camouflaged with mud, leaves, and sticks. When travelers came down the road, a sergeant would accost the group in broken Common as the rest of the war party emerged, surrounding their prey with twenty arrows notched and ready to fire. If the travelers refused to yield, the orcs loosed their arrows and a Ring-nose shaman would cast silence and darkness on any spellcasters in the group. If one of their victims happened to tumble into one of the pit traps, orcs would rush forward with pikes to trap the victim in the hole.

Base of Operations
These orcs dwelt in the swamps of the Bottomless Bogs region of Vaasa. In the late 1360s DR, their raiding parties operated out of the ruined settlement of Moortown.

Possessions
The Ring-nose orcs wore rusted chainmail and steel helmets and used a variety of weapons, including cruel-looking black pikes, short swords, and shortbows. They carried wooden shields emblazoned with their symbol—a black circle—representing the iron rings they wore in their noses.

Relationships
In the mid-to-late 13 century DR, the Ring-nose tribe served the Cult of the Dragon.

History
In the 1360s DR, agents of the Cult of the Dragon killed the tribe's chief and intimidated their warriors with dragons, effectively taking control of the tribe and installing the half-orc cultist Malunko as their new leader. Under his watch, the tribe trained and drilled in ambush and shock tactics, and prepared to march to war across the Galena Mountains and into Thar, with the Cult offering a half-hearted promise of plunder as motivation. Maluko was impatient with the orcs, and on one occasion punished two warriors for being too slow to follow his commands by having his young black dragon mount, Zzygarn, brutally injure them.

By the, the Ring-nose orcs had been relocated to Moortown, a ruined settlement where the Cult of the Dragon had established an outpost and garrison. There, their females and injured were forced to live in a squalid, ruined stable with beds of filthy straw while their warriors were sent to patrol Dead Man's Walk in groups of a dozen. Maluko himself led a war party of twenty Ring-nose orcs in ambushes along the road, strafing travelers with his mount's acid breath. These ambushes were laid with the intent to capture prisoners to take back to Moortown to be intimidated and conscripted into the Cult. These prisoners were kept in the same wretched stables where the tribe's females lived, and their equipment was stored at the Swamphouse Inn. By this point, the Ring-nose orcs wanted nothing more than to escape the Cult back into the swamps, and to be left alone. Even so, they remained in Moortown out of fear of what the dragons would do to them if they tried to flee. However, they were willing to work with the Cult's enemies if it meant a chance to escape.

Members
Members of the Ring-nose clan spoke Orcish but little or no Common.

## Rienur

Rienur was a sailor aboard the Vigilant circa 1374 DR.

Description
Rienur was a notoriously cantankerous, especially for a halfling. His attitude could put a mad half-orc to shame.

History
Rienur was part of the Vigilant's crew in the  when Captain Lastri Kassireh's ship sailed from the Sword Coast to the nation of Samarach on the Chultan Peninsula. Onboard the ship were several passengers, including Volothamp Geddarm who traveled to meet with a wealthy yuan-ti merchant, Sa'Sani. The voyage was cursed with bad luck and numerous strange occurrences. Food supplies were mysteriously spoiled; freshwater barrels ruptured; one of the crew, a sailor got drunk and drowned; Volo almost got poisoned as his poison soup took the life of Joquin; and Rienur noticed new ropes becaming strangely frayed.

The halfling's life ended when the string of occurrences culminated in a deadly storm. The Vigilant's shipwreck was washed ashore not far from the city of Samargol. Most sailors perished in the storm, while others were captured to be devoured by the local cannibalistic Batiri goblins.

## Ricjolo Tomrase

Ricjolo Tomrase was the Baron of Sespech whose reign ended in 1364 DR, succeeded by Baron Thuragar Foesmasher.

History
Ricjolo Tomrase was notoriously known for being a puppet leader of Sespech, a barony of Chondath, before its secession in the ballot of the Rotting Wars. The man was controlled by Arrabar, to the great displeasure of his subjects. In the, Ricjolo's reign ended after ex-adventurer Thuragar Foesmasher rose to power through a series of bloody assassinations and courtly intrigues. When the new Baron's grab or power was successful, Foesmasher ordered Ricjolo Tomrase to return to his masters in Arrabar. Ricjolo's exile led to a small battle between the armies of Sespech and Chondath in a small village of Arranford but resulted in Sespech's long-desired true independence.

## Ring of the will-o-wisp

The Ring of the will-o-wisp was a unique enchanted ring created by a Silvanite druid Eimhec sometime before 1281 DR.

Description
The ring of the will-o-wisp was a brass piece of jewelry with a piece of mandrake impeded in place of a gem.

Powers
The wearer of the ring could tap into its magics to summon a spray of magical motes of light that stung anyone caught within its  cone. The enchantment could be activated once per day.

History
Long before the, the ring of the will-o-wisp was created by Eimhec of Silvanus in order to give some protection to his underlings. The individual known as Omad the Wise was the ring's first known owner. He was believed to have kept the ring until reaching the title of the archdruid. The item next was passed along to Jontan Dunmoss, who was murdered and robbed by a gang of Malarite cultists who kept the ring for several years. Finally, Hunter Renard, the Malarite owner, was sniped dead by elven sharpshooters somewhere in the northern woods of Faerûn.

In 1281 DR, the ring of the will-o-wisp was found in the Field of Bones of the White Wyrm Iceberg deep in the Sea of Moving Ice. The glacier served as the undead white dragon Icasaracht's lair.

## Rivals (short story)

Rivals is a short story written by Richard Lee Byers. It was published in Dragon magazine 343, and later republished in The Year of Rogue Dragons Omnibus. It narrates the first encounter between Dorn Graybrook and Raryn Snowstealer.

Summary
Raryn Snowstealer and Dorn Graybrook travel independently to Pilver's Creek in the Moonsea to pursue a bounty for a white dragon. After Dorn saves Raryn from a band of bullies, he explains that the people of the Moonsea are prejudiced against nonhumans; despite his advice, Raryn decides to try and pursue the bounty anyway. The local mayor, Stevvin Hightower, is reluctant to hire Dorn, who is a fugitive bondsman who has slain his master; however, having seen him in the arena before, he agrees to hire him. Raryn pleads a case that he is a better tracker, and that he could find the dragon; Stevvin decides to hire both, and pay them according to their relative contributions. They are joined by Stevvin and a small band of Red Plume warriors.

Along the way, Raryn attempts to teach Dorn some tracking skills, and later reveals that rather than a dragon, the creature is a cryohydra. Stevvin still needs it slain; they find the reptile slightly torpid, and manage to slay it quickly; after its death, Stevvin turns on them and captures the two, intent on claiming both the bounties for the dragon and for Dorn. His men capture Raryn as well; the dwarf warns them that the hydra has a mate that may return at any moment, but the warning goes unheeded. Raryn and Dorn manage to free themselves, but while they try to sneak out, the cryohydra returns, and they decide to stay and fight; after the battle, Raryn and Dorn take Stevvin hostage, claim their reward, and flee together by sailboat.

## Riverbank Theater

The Riverbank Theater was a large playhouse located in the city of Zhentil Keep during the late 14 century DR.

Location
The guildhall was located in south Zhentil Keep in the central section of the Foreign Quarter just north of Zhent Avenue.

Description
The acoustics within the theater were perfect for projecting sound. The Riverbank Theater could seat 2,500 patrons at a time. Globes of colored glass enchanted with continual light illuminated the plush interior.

Activities
The theater put on nightly performance by bards, jugglers, dancers, jesters, etc. A popular play featured here was Cyric the Duck Sun.

History
Before the destruction of the city in the, the structure was used to house humanoid armies employed by Zhentil Keep. After the city's destruction it lay abandoned for a brief period of time before being taken over by a group of bards and performers who could not escape the city. Upon hearing of the bards' occupation of the building, Lord Orgauth hired artists to restore the building so it could put on proper shows.

## Rising Ride

The Rising Ride was a road that was located in the city of Waterdeep.

Location
The Rising Ride was located in the central section of Waterdeep's Southern Ward. The road originated in the southwest corner of Caravan Court where it branched southwest and eventually turned west to end on the High Road. The Rising Ride was also intersected by Tulmaster's Street, Robin's Way, Juth Alley, and Tornsar Alley.

Notable Locations
Coach & Wagon Hall and Saddlers' & Harness-Makers' Hall sat at opposite sides of the intersection of Tulmaster's Street with the Rising Ride. The House of Good Spirits sat along the Rising Ride where it was joined by Robin's Way.

History
The road earned its named from a small knoll that sat along its length, causing the road to elevate. The House of Good Spirits was founded at the top of this rise.

## Ring of invisibility

A ring of invisibility was an enchanted ring that allowed the wearer to turn invisible at will.

Description
It appeared to be an unremarkable silver ring, some of the rings were decorated with agates.

Powers
When they so wished, the wearer of this ring could turn invisible, as per the spell invisibility. The power of these rings functioned automatically whenever the wearer desired so long as the ring was worn. One in ten rings of invisibility also made the wearer inaudible. A wearer of one of the inaudible variants could speak if they desired, but would cease the inaudible function of the ring for that moment.

They remained in this state until they removed the ring, cast a spell, or attacked any person or creature.

The ring had an unlimited number of uses.

A cursed ring of invisibility could simply disappear at inopportune times or make only the wearer invisible, not their clothing and carried items.

Creation
To make a ring of invisibility, one required skill in forging rings and the spell invisibility. The silver used should also be pure or from an existing ring of invisibility.

Variations
Before around  about one-in-ten rings had inaudibility as well as invisibility, making the wearer absolutely silent as well. If the wearer wished to speak they would break this feature to do so.

Notable Rings
 Sandthief's ring: A number of these rings were used by the Waterdhavian master thief known as the Sand Thief.

Notable Users
 Balian Rocksmith: A dwarven guard who discovered a unique ioun stone called Balian's yellow ioun stone.
 Catherine Kincaid: Captain of the Harbor Patrol of Ravens Bluff in the 14th century DR, had a ring of invisibility and inaudibility both.
 Crelt: A blind gnomish clairsentient.
 Ferret Jones: An unlucky smuggler who tangled with the Zhentarim in the 14 century DR.
 Nymara Scheiron, one of the Lords of Waterdeep circa the .
 Tipsy Toehammer: An extremely lucky but lazy halfling thief, active in the 14 century DR.
 Zain, a spy and assassin in service of the ruler of Hillsfar circa 1374 DR.
 Tevlo Spath, a private detective of Spath Investigations in Ravens Bluff in the mid-to-late 1300s DR.
 Senior members of the Church of Mask often wore rings of invisibility.

## Ring of clumsiness

Ring of clumsiness or Jester's Folly was a cursed ring that made the wearer excessively clumsy.

Powers
Like other cursed objects, rings of clumsiness could only be taken off via remove curse spell, a powerful cleric, or other similar means. Unless removed, these rings turned the wearer into an unlucky klutz, making dextrous tasks such as hiding in shades, stealing, or disarming traps extremely challenging actions.

History
In the, a ring of clumsiness could be found within a massive Aurilian tempe erected by her cultist, allied with the Legion of the Chimera in Icewind Dale.

As of the, Jester's Folly was worn by an ill-humored Zhentish mage need Niemain who operated on the second floor of Sorcerous Sundries in Baldur's Gate.

Notable Rings of Clumsiness
One notable Jester's Folly ring belonged to Gregoria the Foole who made a living exploiting its curse. Gregoria was a jester who called unlucky audience members on stage and sneakily replaced one of their rings with Jester's Folly. Gregoria entertained the crowd by mocking the victim's crippling clumsiness to uproarious applause and laughter. When the performance was over, Gregoria the Foole switched the ring back (by revealed secret means) but only after the victim swore of no reprisals for public humiliation. Gregoria's last performance was in Zhentil Keep when a certain ill-humored mage did not appreciate being mocked.

## Riluaneth

Riluaneth was an inhabitant of Cormanthyr in the 3 century DR.

Appearance
He was rather tall and muscular, for an elf.

Personality
His streak of cruelty was almost controlling.

Relationships
He was resentful, and delighted in teasing and tormenting his cousins.

History
Riluaneth was taken in by the Alastrarra family after his parents died.

On, when Elminster Aumar came to the Alastrarra house transformed into Iymbryl Alastrarra and wearing his kiira, he stumbled upon Riluaneth playing a game of spellcircles with a maid. He sent a blade towards Elminster, which he dodged, only to find its true quarry was a servant; Elminster then stole control of the blade. While the two fought mentally over control of the sword, Riluaneth reached for a dagger; rather than risk the magic in it, Elminster cast a spell which Iymbryl had stored in the kiira for precisely such a time. The spell slew Riluaneth on the spot.

## Rite of Pain and Purity

History
The Rite of Pain and Purity was used by Loviatans to celebrate the four main seasonal festivals. During the celebration, clergy members assembled in a circle to dance while singing and chanting and standing upon broken glass, barbed wire, thorns, or other painful objects. Participants were encouraged to greater degrees of revelry at the ends of whips wielded by ranking priests. It was possible for a red ring of radiance to form above the circle, allowing Loviatar to appear to speak of a special mission. The Rite would last for half an hour if Loviatar did not apear, at which point the priests would heal their injuries.

## River Gate (Waterdeep)

The River Gate was the eastern point of entrance through the Trollwall into Waterdeep, City of Splendors.

Description
The gate featured a single tower on either side of River Street. Each tower stood four stories tall.

Location
The River Gate was found in eastern Waterdeep in the southeast section of the Trades Ward.

Law & Order
Any large caravan that wished to enter through River Gate had to register with one of the Magisters of Waterdeep, who was always accompanied by members of the City Guard. Small parties and individuals only had to register with one if their stay extended beyond a tenday. In both cases, discovery of a failure to do so would result in either a fine or being put to forced labor.

Defenses
Prior to the, as a defensive measure one of the Walking Statues of Waterdeep were stationed beneath the River Gate and hidden away on the Ethereal plane. It actively defended against ethereal intrusion, attacking any ethereal creatures it perceived within Waterdeep's bounds, but would ignore ethereal humanoids unless there was a group of ten or more of them. In 1385 DR, it and six of the other hidden Walking Statues exited the Ethereal plane and left their positions to rampage across the city.

## Ritiik

The ritiik was a member of the spear family of polearms, used in the polar regions of Toril, such as the Great Glacier.

Description
The weapons were made of bone and measured  in length, with a weight of around . In addition to a pointed spear head, ritiiks had an additional hook-like blade that protruded from the spearhead's base. In some lands, this weapon could sell for as much as 5 gold pieces.

Weaponology
The ritiik was designed for the purpose of attacking large animals found in cold environs, such as polar bears. Whenever the shaft of this weapon managed to pierce the flesh of a creature, the spear head would then be jerked backed so that its hook could dig into the flesh, potentially allowing for them to be dragged down to the ground.

Notable Users
Individuals

 Najass, a master Iulutiun hunter, carried a ritiik.
 Uhokkaki, a highly regarded warrior and pimataung of the Hupiik tribe, carried a ritiik.

Races & Sentient Creatures
 The ritiik was predominantly used in the Great Glacier by the Iulutiun people, including the Angulutiun sub-group, being a common weapon among hunting parties.
 Beyond the Iulutiuns, the weapon could be found in use by some neanderthals.

Religions
 The ritiik was a common weapon of the priests and specialty priests of Ulutiu.

## Ring of projection

A ring of projection was a magical ring that aided in all manner of throwing- and launching-related tasks.

Description
The ring appeared as a plain and unremarkable brass ring.

Powers
The ring allowed the wearer to throw an object (weapon or otherwise) three times the normal distance, while missiles launched or fired by the wearer also had their potential range tripled. The speed of the augmented missile was dramatically increased, inflicting additional damage on impact and reaching a far more distant target in the same time as a closer, mundane shot. This power did not require anything more than intent to activate, though placing distant shots on-target did still require practice and care to aim properly.

A less-obvious quality of the ring caused a small pocket of air to be carried along with the launched munition, allowing it to carry air to submerged beings or allow an incendiary missile to remain lit when it would otherwise be extinguished due to wind or water.

A secondary power could be activated by speaking a command word engraved on the inner side of the band. The wearer could imbue themselves or someone else they touched with the enhancements of jump for a single leap. This power could only be given to a single target at a time, but did allow them to jump great distances while carrying a heavy load. All of the ring's powers became inactive for a few seconds after each time jump was used.

## Rielev

Rielev was a tortured experiment captured by the cruel archmage named Jon Irenicus some years before 1369 DR.

History
Rielev was trapped in a torturous existence within a glass chamber sometime before the  and kept in Jon Irenicus's Dungeon and laboratory deep underneath the city of Athkatla in Amn. Once, Rielev was a loyal servant to Jon Irenicus. The poor wretch's existence was clouded in oblivion and ambiguity, stuck between life and death. According to their clouded memory, Irenicus was a friend and of the same people as the tortured experiment. Rielev was promised eternal life and youth in reward for their service; until then, Irenicus suspended them in a glass chamber, eventually forgetting or not caring for Rielev's fate. They asked for death and freedom from unending torture. The unfortunate soul's life was fueled by an energy source. Rielev asked Abdel Adrian, who recently escaped Irenicus's clutches, to remove the batteries, thus ending their existence.

The ring of form retention ensured that a shapechanging effect — whether from a wizard's spell or a shapechanger's innate ability — remained uninterrupted and intact through circumstances that could negatively affect it. Thus, it was highly prized by those who used magical disguises. The ring was powerful enough to maintain the changed form of a dead doppelganger, which would normally revert to their natural form immediately on death.

## Rikard Dolban

Lieutenant Rikard Dolban, known as the Black Baron of Tethyr, was an infamous thug who terrorized peasants during the Tethyrian Interregnum.

Possessions
Rikard had a black chainmail gauntlet, which he stole from the royal armory. For this armored glove, he demanded that he be called the Black Baron.

Activities
The Black Baron used his stolen black gauntlet while torturing and questioning prisoners of his several employers.

History
Rikard hailed from the town of Tethammar, which was outside Castle Tethyr. He joined the town guard, simply so that he could wield power over his neighbors. When Castle Tethyr fell at the start of the Ten Black Days of Eleint in the, he saw it as an opportunity for a rise in power. He happened to be on his first night watch at the time. As the castle burned, he and eight companions stole many weapons, slew their commanding officers, and joined the looters. He stole many treasures from the castle, including his namesake gauntlet and a prize stallion.

For several years, the Black Baron and his men raided, murdered, and raped villagers in the plains around the Tethyruins and the Starspire Mountains. After growing bored of life as a bandit, he offered his services as an enforcer to several of the petty rulers of Tethyr at that time. He began working for Minshak Keseri, a wizard, in 1350 DR, then for Pasha Balik, who ruled the city of Zazesspur until 1364 DR. Next, Baron Dolban worked as strong-arm for Baron Lutwill. Lutwill was killed by adventuress Zaranda Star in 1366 DR, and Rikard's final employer was Baron Zam.

Baron Zam was one of the nobles who betrayed Zaranda Star, after having sworn an oath to support her rise to power over the nation. Zam and Rikard joined forces instead with Yusuf Jhannivvar. During the Second Siege of Myratma, in the, Rikard tried to flee the city, but he was captured by Onyx the Invincible and Estemal Talltankard.

Rikard Dolban was hanged for treason four months after the siege had ended.

## Rian Forbeck

Rian Forbeck was a traveler and an author who penned the book, The Approachable East, some time during or before the late 15 century DR.

Personality
Rian dreamt of visiting the lands of Rashemen, home of the mysterious Wychlaran and mighty Rashemaar berserkers.

History
At some point in their life, Rian embarked upon their journey to the lands of the Unapproachable East, with the ultimate goal of visiting Rashemen.

Rian's journey took them to the lands of Thesk, a land of profound tolerance that acted as a great melting pot of contrasting cultures. They continued along the famed Golden Way, where they witnessed goods and peoples from the distant Hordelands and far off continent of Kara-Tur. Ignorant of those lands and their people, Rian was truly impressed, and wondered if their original dream was om fact rather ordinary.

While camping near the great Golden Way, Rian was approached by a traveler, who invited the author to sit with the Council of Zulkirs in the nation of Thay. Rian could hardly refuse such a kind offer, and agreed to put off their visit to Rashemen just a bit longer, and head first to nearby Thay.

## Rissik

A rissik was a mobile structure used by iskotuk of the Ulutiuns.

Description
A rissik was a unique tent structure composed of four to eight ribs measuring  in length. These ribs were secured together with leather cords, forming the framework of the tent. The rissik was designed to open like an umbrella, with its ribs embedded into the ground, creating a dome-shaped tent with a flat roof. When not in use, the rissik was collapsed into a compact bundle. Despite its size, a bundled rissik was lightweight and was easily carried by an adult caribou, with several tents in tow.

The ribs of the rissik were constructed from thin and flexible pieces of wood or bone. To provide shelter, the tent was covered with two layers of caribou skin. The skins were arranged in such a way that the furry sides touched each other, creating an enclosed pocket of air. This air pocket served as insulation, trapping warmth within the tent. The skins were stretched tightly over the ribs to ensure that the air pocket remained open and effective in providing insulation.

The interior of a rissik was not as warm as a snowhouse. However, properly constructed rissik could still be up to 30 °F (17 °C) warmer within than outside.

Construction
The first step in erecting a rissik was selecting an appropriate site. The optimal sites for setting up the rissik were within forested areas or on thick ice. These locations offered the advantage of reduced snow drifting issues compared to other areas. The presence of trees or solid ice helped mitigate the risk of snow accumulation and provided a more secure and stable environment for the rissik.

The most unfavorable locations were those found in areas covered with freshly fallen snow or near hills and mountains. In such places, the rissik was susceptible to snow drifting from banks or being blown off the slopes, leading to its complete burial overnight. In the best-case scenario, the camper would need to spend their morning digging themselves out of the snow, but in the worst-case scenario, there was a risk of suffocation.

The second step was to dig the holes for the ribs. The holes should have a depth of approximately  and be slightly wider than the diameter of a rib. Typically, a knife or hukek was sufficient for digging these holes. However, if the ground proved to be particularly hard, the tool might need to be heated beforehand. Some Angulutiuns carried a specialized tool called a tihukek, which was a hollow bone placed upright on the ground at the desired location for the rib hole. The camper then poured a small amount of boiling water into the tihukek, causing the ground to thaw and facilitating easier digging.

The third step was to secure the ribs. The rissik was carefully unfolded and the ribs inserted into their respective holes. The covering was smoothed to ensure a taut fit. The holes were then packed with snow and water poured over the packed snow. Once the water froze, the ribs were secured in place.

The fourth step was the construction of a windbreak. When camping in open plains, it was essential to protect against potential burial by drifts, especially during snowfall at night. To mitigate this risk, a windbreak was built approximately  from the rissik, facing the direction of the wind. The windbreak was often built of snow and ice and was about  tall forming a half-circle. The windbreak was strengthened by pouring water over it, which quickly froze.

The fifth step was to ensure the roof was secure. A separate section of skin, known as a sarissik, functioned as the roof of the rissik. The sarissik was attached to the top edge of the rissik using leather cord, leaving a small opening for ventilation.

The sixth step was to construct a bed platform. The bed platform in a rissik, made of ice blocks and covered with skins, was identical to that found in snowhouses. In the absence of ice blocks, snow or branches served as alternatives.

The final step was to illuminate the interior using a small lamp or a cooking pot filled with blubber. The fire was covered with an uliririssik to protect the walls of the rissik.

## Ring moss

Ring moss was a type of rare moss found within crevasses near the Lugsaas Chain of the Great Glacier.

Uses
Ring moss was delicately ground into a soft paste and was applied to coat arrows or other weapons. This paste acted as a powerful sedative tirichik. When a tirichik was struck by a weapon coated with ring moss, it fell unconsciousness for a time. The potency of the ring moss paste remained intact for a period of up to four days following its harvesting.

## Riddlemaster

Riddlemasters were exceptionally intelligent bards that devoted themselves to intellectual pursuits, becoming masters at conceiving and solving all manner of riddles, puzzles, and rhymes.

Activities
All riddlemasters shared the view that daily life could be navigated like any puzzle. From this mindset they sought to advance their intellectual understanding and analyze the odds and probabilities in any given situation so as to determine the actions necessary for achieving a favorable outcome, such as what were the best questions to ask and who to focus on in a fight.

Evil-aligned riddlemasters sought to demean and put down others, good ones sought to educate people, and others simply reveled in poking fun at other people. In general, many riddlemasters liked to flaunt their intellectual skills by speaking in riddles or rhymes and they loved to pose questions to commoners in the form of carefully constructed riddles. These riddle questions would conceal either lessons or observations that applied to the those being questioned. It was also custom among riddlemasters to not explain what seemed obvious to them unless asked direct, specific questions, but instead only offer one or two observations so that those around them could work their way to the conclusion themselves.

Though most riddlemasters were too absorbed in intellectual pursuits to cultivate other skills, some became skilled in bartering, gambling, or the art of being a jeweler.

Culture
Some believed that the skills displayed by riddlemasters were nothing more than extraordinary luck, much like the luck commonly displayed by jesters. Also like jesters, many commoners labeled riddlemasters as being crazy due to their strange actions and behavior. Their way of living tended to irritate commoners and fellow adventurers alike, necessitating a mobile lifestyle.

Abilities
Riddlemasters were more proficient than the average bard in learning new spells and using spell scrolls or other other magic items that were in written form, such as books or maps.

Equipment
In terms of weaponry a riddlemaster favored those that required some level of finesse to use, such as blowguns, bows and crossbows, daggers, darts, hand axes, javelins, quarterstaves, slings and staff slings, spears, short swords, and whips.

History
By the, riddlemasters were considered to be relatively rare in Faerûn.

Some time during or prior to 1364 DR, the riddle-hoarding green dragon Grimnoshtadrano destroyed the village of Taskerleigh near Waterdeep and from it took the magical harp Morninglark, which he carried back to his lair in the High Forest. Soon thereafter, Grimnosh was approached by the spellsinger Iriador Wintermist, under the alias of "Garnet", with a proposition: if she could have the Morninglark, she would use it to issue a widely spread challenge for adventurers and riddlemasters to approach Grimnosh with their best riddles. Grimnosh agreed and received a scroll from Garnet to give to anyone who succeeded at the challenge.

Soon enough, a rag-tag army of adventurers, minstrels, and mercenaries led by Danilo Thann and Elaith Craulnober, accompanied by the riddlemaster Vartain, sought out the lair of Grimnosh in order to obtain the Morninglark for themselves.

Notable Riddlemasters
 Elvira of the Bluff, a Ravenian half-elf bard and mage, was referred to by some as being a riddlemaster.
 Vartain, a widely traveled human riddlemaster that hailed from Calimshan.

Rings of charm were minor enchanted rings that allowed the wearer to bewitch humanoids.

These bands of precious metal were inlaid with tiny glowing gemstones. The wearer of such ring was able to cast the charm person spell once per day. Additionally, the ring enhanced the wearer's charisma.

History
In the, a ring of charm was worn by Dolon Daemba, an envoy from the Legion of the Chimera to the Black Raven Monastery. Dolon, presumably, used the ring's magic to sway the Aruma Blane's mind on getting involved with the Legion's war against the Ten Towns.

## Rite of Profane Ascension

The Rite of Profane Ascension was an evil, ancient ritual that allowed a vampiric being to amplify its strengths and shed its weaknesses (such as sunlight hypersensitivity).

Description
The ritual entailed incantations in the Infernal language and a sacrifice of seven thousand souls, including all of the vampire's spawn. Once it had been carried out, the person leading it would become the Vampire Ascendant. In their new form, they would no longer be affected by the usual curses afflicting vampires, such as sensitivity to sunlight or running water. Additionally, all the powers previously lent to them by their vampire form would grow even more powerful.

History
The origins of the rite were lost in time. As of, the only being to know the details of it was the archdevil Mephistopheles, who, in the 13 century DR, brokered an infernal contract with the vampire lord Cazador Szarr. Likewise, Cazador (or potentially through him, his spawn, Astarion Ancunín) was the only vampire known by name to attempt performing the ritual.

History
Historically, rings of flinging were used by organizations like the Harpers, who often preferred to stay out of a blade's reach and often found themselves chasing or being chased across the rooftops during moonlit nights. These rings allowed the Harper agents to end wrongdoers with good aim and a loose roof tile. In the, one such ring was being offered for sale by Arron, a lightfoot halfling druid traders of the Emerald Grove in the Western Heartlands.

The wearer of the ring could tap into the exhort the risen enchantment once per day, which acted like the command undead spell. ## Ringfork Stronghold

The Ringfork Stronghold was an orcish fortification in the Trielta Hills of the Western Heartlands in the late 15 century DR.

History
By the, the Ringfork Stronghold was ruled by Lord Heradd, who was believed to have had his sights on usurping leadership of the two other orcish communities of the Trielta Hills. The fort was approached by an agent of the Cult of the Absolute in the, who sought to recruit Lord Heradd's orcs into the cult's army. Agent Mhoss, the first envoy, was dismembered by Lord Heradd's guard—an act that led to the cult's re-evaluation of their strategy. Sir Yax of Scornubel was decided to be the next envoy as he was familiar with orcish traditions and culture. Yax's strategy was to arrive accompanied by two ogres and issue a personal challenge to Lord Heradd for the killing of Agent Mhoss. Yax then would have to defeat the orcish lord, force him to swear allegiance to the Absolute, and, in exchange, the cult would offer Lord Heradd an alliance in conquering the other two orcish communities of the Trielta Hills.

By the, these common pieces of enchanted jade were found across many pockets, fingers, and merchant stalls of the Sword Coast. They were especially popular among adventurers, freeing their hand for weapons and tools instead of holding lanterns and torches. As of the, rings of jade were sold at Okuzo's Imports, a shop that dealt with exotic Kara-Turan goods, located on the Harbor Loop in Westgate.

## Rivington General

The Rivington General was a smithy shop that produced quality weapons and armor, made by Gyldro Angleiron, its master smith and proprietor in the late 15 century DR.

Location
The Rivington General stood on the Coast Way in Rivington, just south across the road from the Open Hand Temple.

Structure

The smithy and the shop were two connected buildings. The shop was used exclusively by the customers and run by a clerk, while the smithy, to the south, was Gyldro's home. Customers were not welcome in the workshop.

History
Master Gyldro Angleiron purchased Rivington General from its previous owner, old Gossoon, sometime in or shortly before the .

In the, Master Angleiron started seeing weapons forged out of infernal iron appearing in the city of Baldur's Gate. Once, a scimitar made of this exotic metal was brought to the Rivington General by a Rivington Rats gang member. That was the third item of the kind. The smith swiftly sold the weapons on to get them out of his shop, believing that these diabolic items would bring nothing but trouble. He, however, wondered where the infernal iron was coming from all of a sudden. The same year, Gyldro was attacked and knocked out by one of the True Soul heroes of Baldur's Gate. In reality, it was a shapeshifting Chosen of Bhaal, Orin the Red. She briefly impersonated Master Gyldro Angleiron to taunt the True Soul heroes, only to subsequently abduct and murder the man, leaving store clerk and aspiring smith Exxvikyap managing the shop by herself.

In the, Lady Madeline Whitburn of the Whitburn family of the Upper City, made a discrete purchase of Rigid-Quik Cordial via her butler, Blathers, to aid Lord "Whit" Whitburn's problem of "stiffness". Lady Whitburn used double the recommended dosage, mixing it with a glass of evening sherry to absolutely no effect. Madeline mailed a complaint letter to the proprietors of Bonecloak's Apothecary demanding money back or an alternative potion that would aid Lord Whitburn. ## Ring ward

A ring ward was a type of magical protection used in the kingdom of Cormyr. It was the weakest of three standard types of wards employed by the king. Like each of the three standard wards, the actual spell to prepare the ward was a carefully guarded state secret, though it was similar to the spell known as wardmist.

Description
A ring ward would apply a weak electrical shock to any person not bearing a Purple Dragon ring. The shock was strong enough to turn away various animals as well. In addition, any such unauthorized creature would trigger a light or sound alarm.

The ward also inhibited the casting of weaker spells in the vicinity.

The palace in the city of Arabel had several floors protected by ring wards, as did the gates of Oldstone Hall in Wheloon.

## Rivenaurlgoth

Rivenaurlgoth the Darkly Pious was a red great wyrm  who lived in the Marching Mountains of Calimshan in the 3 century before Dalereckoning. He was the most powerful servant of Bane of the day.

History
In the, followers of the god Anachtyr killed the dragon, took its skull and gilded it in gold, and carved the Myth of the Four Rivers into its teeth. Later, Banites stole the skull and kept it hidden to preserve their deity's fearsome reputation. Over time, the followers of Bane forgot exactly why they were hiding Rivenaurlgoth's skull, but they still felt compelled to keep it so out of greed. As of, the skull had passed into the hands of worshipers of Cyric, who kept it in the Mountain of Skulls in Tethyr. Rivenaurlgoth's skull was considered a particularly holy item of the Church of Cyric, with access restricted to only one worshiper at a time.

At a later date, he and Vonda tried to rob a nobleman in Waterdeep, but he was captured, interrogated, and tortured to death. During this, he revealed the names of the rest of the Brightstar Sash; the nobleman's forces came after them, forcing the Sash to flee Waterdeep.

## Rick Brill

Rick Brill was a game designer who has worked for the Role Playing Game Association network as the Campaign Director of Information for the Living City by 2001 and was among game testers for the 4 edition of Dungeons & Dragons. Before that, Rick was a member of the Grevbeards, an RPGA players' group that participated in the Living City program as players in 1995 and 1996. As of 1998, Rick's player character was Sir Jean-Dorial Encarthan, Knight Commander of the Griffon Guard.

Richard was born in Superior, WI to Roger and Jann Brill on October 7, 1970. He graduated from the University of Minnesota-Duluth with business and english degrees. He married Toni Winslow in 2006, with whom he had a daughter – Samantha. Rick took the position of IT Director for Labovitz School of Business and Economics in 2010. He passed away on September 27, 2015, at the age of 44 after suffering a tragic accent in Hermanton, MN. Richard was buried in Lake Nebagamon Cemetery.

Works
Rick penned several Living City adventure modes, including For Sale in 1998, On Guard and Before Its Time in 1999. Apart from Living City, Rick contributed to Living Greyhawk, including Dorakaa adventure module.

## Rightful Conspiracy

The Rightful Conspiracy was an initiative in the early 1370s DR amongst the nobility of Cormyr to overthrow Regent Alusair Obarskyr and assassinate Filfaeril Obarskyr so that a new king could ascend to the throne. It fizzled before the end of the year.

Organization
The Conspiracy was chaotic and porous, largely lacking in organization, with numerous members working towards different goals and some even trying to murder the others. However, the early spring meeting was held at an island chosen for its defensible position, requiring the crossing of three guard posts and two bridges to reach it. Over time, evidence of backing from Sembia and Amn surfaced.

History
In early spring of the, a meeting was joined in Marsember, where numerous merchants, local Marsembans, Cormyrean nobles, and even some Red Wizards in disguise got together to discuss a future assassination of the ruling monarchy of Cormyr and its replacement by a different king. The meeting was infiltrated by Elminster, too, and ultimately broke down after a barge full of War Wizards barged into the island, bypassing the defenses entirely. Elminster also attacked some of the Red Wizards, completely halting all activity on the isle.

In the aftermath, the thief Narnra Shalace of Waterdeep was caught in the crossfire after accidentally following Elminster into Marsember, culminating in her being pursued for questioning afterwards. She ultimately was hired by Caladnei to scout out a number of War Wizards who'd met with members of the Conspiracy.

More activity related to the Rightful Conspiracy followed later that year, as evidence of Sembian and Amnian backing was discovered by the War Wizards. The Conspiracy's culmination was to be a massed attack at Thundaerlyn Hall, where a number of nobles would personally attempt to slay Alusair and Filfaeril. Unfortunately for them, the magical backup they were expecting from the Red Wizards was thwarted by the sudden arrival of a flight of dragons, which included Ammaratha Cyndusk and Aeglyl Dreadclaw among others. During the fight, Queen Filfaeril's teleport gem was destroyed by a young scion of the Dracohorn family, preventing their escape. However, thanks to the help of Noumea Cardellith, Filfaeril's Highknights endured long enough for Glarasteer Rhauligan to arrive with reinforcements.

Members
Members indicted/slain after first meeting
 Beltrar Morgrin, War Wizard
 Thauvas Zlorn, a.k.a. "Khornadar of Westgate", Red Wizard (Starangh's direct liaison with the Conspiracy), slain after the meeting

Members released after first meeting
 Amrelle Mistwind, who was just "on her nightly manhunt"

Others involved in first meeting
 Harnrim Starangh, Red Wizard (coordinated with the Red Wizards)
 Tharmoraera, pleasure-lass of Marsember
 Lord Blackwinter, noble
 A Thorntower scion
 Sorval Maethur, assassinated during the meeting
 Imbur Waendlar, furniture merchant

Members involved in attempted assassination
 Thandro
 Sauvrurn
 Darndreth Goldsword
 A scion of the Dracohorn family
 Harnrim Starangh (attempted to time an attack on Vangerdahast to take advantage of the conspiracy)

Other members
 Telarantra, War Wizard of Cormyr (attempted to assassinate Vangerdahast)

## Ring of mental inhibition

Rings of mental inhibition were simple enchanted rings that were created sometime long before the late 15 century DR.

Powers
The gemstone inlaid in the ring's band displayed swirling metallic patterns that could leave an observer dizzy and lightheaded. This effect enhanced the wearer's spells, making targets who failed to resist spell effects feel mental fatigue for twelve seconds, making them vulnerable to spells and effects that affected their mental capacities.

History
As of the, a ring of mental inhibition could be found in a locked chest among spoils and ruins of the battlefield on the outskirts of the town of Reithwin, left behind from the final battle between the Harper forces and the cultists of General Ketheric Thorm in the late 14 century DR.

## Rivet

Rivets were metal fasteners that were used to join fabrics, leather, metal, and other materials. They belonged to a broad class of hardware that included bolts, nails, and spikes.

Description
Rivets somewhat resembled nuts and bolts, though instead of combining two separate components a rivet was a single piece, like a nail. A particularly durable and resilient version of rivet was made from an adamantine alloy.

Usage
 Brigandine armor was created by riveting metal plates onto fabric or leather layers, creating a cheap and effective composite armor. The combined plates of field plate were typically held together by rivets, as were the component strips of metal used in splint mail. The curiously effective studded leather armor also used rivets, though instead of holding protective metal plates together, the rivets themselves were the defense.WARNING: The use of rivets as a primary defensive feature, as in studded leather, is impractical and ahistorical, and should not be attempted in real life. Taking a strong impact upon a rivet attached to flexible materials such as a soft leather jerkin may result in severe bruising or cuts as the rivet is driven mercilessly into your flesh.
 After wire was coiled into rings on a lathe and combined into interleaved links, tiny rivets would be used to pin the loops closed, creating chain mail.
 Thaluud stone armor was a peculiar form of armor made for tomb tappers to wear. It consisted of large stone plates held together with rivets and hinges.
 The Temple of Gond in Bezantur was made out of enormous iron plates held together with massive rivets, among other features.

History
Rivets were an early human invention, being used by the ancient Netherese and Calishites.

## Ritual of renaming

Ritual of renaming was a spell that forever transformed the creature's true name.

Effects
A successful ritual of renaming completely removed all connection between the target and its prior true name, giving it a new true name in its stead. This new truename was known only to the caster, the target, and those attending.

Components
Alongside verbal components and somatic components, the ritual called for a loss to the caster's self, their energy, their experience, and it demanded the subject's true name be spoken correctly, which was often a taxing truespeak task. If the true name was not spoken correctly, the spell failed.

History
This spell was necessary in order to become a bereft.

Ritual axes were kept shiny and polished, but they ended up splattered in caked-on blood regardless.

Ritual axes possessed the Scourge Mistress' bane, an enchantment that affected the wielder and their targets. Every landed slash with the axe had a chance of worsening the aim of the opponent and making them more vulnerable to mundane and magical effects. However, unless the axe's wielder was severely wounded, each attack with it cut and bled the wielder.

## Riven Stone

The Riven Stone was a landmark in Faerûn.

The landmark was an immense pitch-black helm-like conical stone, cloven down the middle, through which the road went.

The Riven Stone was down a road from Anthather's Mill, in the midst of a forest.

In the, Elminster Aumar, a Chosen of Mystra, sought out the Riven Stone as part of a quest commanded by the goddess Mystra. There, he met Dasumia, whom he was commanded by the goddess to serve.

## Ripplestones

Ripplestones was a village on the Dragon Coast between Westgate and Starmantle in the mid–8 century DR.

Description
Ripplestones had a bridge on the Westgate side; it was uneven and rough, with a sign on the Ripplestones side. Past the bridge, the main road of the village turned around Scorchstone Hall, a small manor surrounded by a thicket of trees and thorny bushes. The cottages of Caladaster and Baerdagh were close to it. There was a tavern, the Fair Maid. In particular, Baerdagh passed the bend in the road by Scorchstone when going from his house to the Fair Maid.

Geography
The road towards Starmantle went along the coast for some miles.

History
Elminster Aumar, a Chosen of Mystra, went to Ripplestones in the ; there, he shared a meal with two local elderly men, Caladaster and Baerdagh, from whom he learned about Scorchstone Hall, where the sorceress Sharindala had dwelt. Later, the elven mage Ilbryn Starym came from Westgate too, pursuing Elminster; he left as soon as he learned where the man was. Three people arrived later, the scholars Tabarast and Beldrune, accompanied by a nameless Harper who was also seeking Elminster; they too left after a single meal, accompanied by Caladaster.

Days later, the High Duke of Langalos, Horostos, sent an immense caravan out to see if the Slayer would be daunted or somehow stopped by a force of that size. The caravan stopped at Ripplestones, just in time for Sharindala to come to the Fair Maid.

Rumors & Legends
It was said that Sharindala still walked the halls of Scorchstone Hall. This was true, but it was also said she was a moaning spirit, when Sharindala was actually mute because of her undeath.

Notable Inhabitants
 Jalobal
 Alnyskawer

Appearances

## Ringyl

The Ringyl was a castle in Tresset's Ringyl.

Location
Tresset's Ringyl was within a day's march from Felmorel. The town holding the castle was itself nestled in a valley between six hills.

Structure
The castle's towers were slender spires. It had tall battlements, and a wall.

Interior
Across one of the Ringyl's crumbling walls, there was a room that resembled a chapel, with a high and vaulted ceiling; the walls inside were painted to resemble trees with gilded fruit, and the floor had wavy bands of malachite. The far wall had a carving of a pair of lips, about as wide as a tall man's outstretched arms, surrounded by 12 or 14 irregular stars. Behind that carving was a hidden coffin on a catafalque. There was an underground level beneath the chapel.

Defenses
As of, the castle's walls were fallen.

History
Once, a bandit by the name of Tresset had raised the castle to anchor a realm he'd dubbed Tressardon. The kingdom didn't survive him for more than a few days. As of the mid–8 century DR, the castle and town were deserted, during Elminster Aumar's wanderings in service of Mystra.

After the battle between Elminster and Silent Antlers, an obscure entity, the chapel inside the Ringyl crumbled apart, and the lips in the chapel shattered, a catafalque bursting out; in tilting over, the coffin fell through the ground into the lower level. Then it exploded. One of the arms of the catafalque, actually an enchanted scepter, was then reclaimed by Dasumia somehow, despite having been turned into dust by the explosion.

## Rinthar's Wagonworks

Rinthar's Wagonworks was a workshop in the settlement of Bargewright Inn in the Savage Frontier in the mid-to-late 14 century DR. It was run by the wheelwright Rinthar and his six apprentices.

Location
It stood in the upper part of Bargewright Inn, on the southern side of the road known as the Back, between the Stalls and Halduth Meer's house.

Services
A skilled wheelwright, Rinthar was most known for crafting plain sturdy wheels. He would steam and bend ash wood into shape out in the workshop's yard. These he sold for 15 gp for one wheel, 25 gp for two.

In addition, he and his six young apprentices repaired wagons, usually to a rough but serviceable quality. A regular repair took about a full day and cost 10 gp for a wagon, while a quick repair could be done by evening or the next morning, but was pricy at 30 gp for a wagon.

Moreover, Rinthar was an expert on wood and could identify even rare or ancient specimens, and furthermore could apply appropriate weatherproofing.

A skilled wheelwright, Rinthar was most known for crafting plain sturdy wheels. He would steam and bend ash wood into shape out in the workshop's yard. In addition, he and his six young apprentices repaired wagons, usually to a rough but serviceable quality.
