History
In the, the army assembled by the Cult of the Absolute moved from the Moonrise Towers in Chionthar Valley to menace the city of Baldur's Gate, displacing countless refugees along the way in the process. Some of the Baldurians did not take kindly to the refugees flooding the outskirts of the city, while others took to protesting. Ricks Garven was among the protestors in Rivington, who thought that the refugee tent city hid treacherous agents of the Absolute in their midst.

## Rite of the Timeless Body

The Rite of the Timeless Body was an esoteric druidic ritual used to prolong a learned and powerful druid's lifespan beyond what was natural for their race.

Description
To a casual observer, the scroll containing the Rite of the Timeless Body appeared to be a confusing collection of strange symbols and sketches. The scroll prepared by the High Harper of Baldur's Gate, Jaheira, was a chaotic collection of druidic scripts and sketches carefully notated but incomplete.

History
By the, Jaheira had spent years but eventually acquired the rare and obscure ritual and deciphered its steps and components. Being a half-elf of over a century in age, she was torn between the desire to continue her work for the betterment of the Gate and coming to terms with her age and eventual end. Jaheira kept the ritual's scroll in her private and secure sanctuary: an underground house and a small cave underneath her home in the Lower City. As the Absolute crisis concluded, Jaheira was at peace with her story, eventually receiving an ending, but just not yet. She hoped that the new generation of heroes would carry the torch and continue protecting the Gate.

## Ricnan Midler

Ricnan Midler was the head of the extended Midler family in the mid-1480s DR, who lived together in the Midler farmhouse near Eveningstar in Cormyr.

Description
Ricnan was a middle-aged man with tied-back brown hair and a neat brown waistcoat outfit.

Relationships
Ricnan was the son of Neris and Ianhand, and brother of Quilynn. He had wed Jandove, and together they had their two sons Normon and Thoben.

History

During the Sschindylryn invasion of Eveningstar, the drow forces of Vicala Szind attacked the farmhouse and captured the entire family alive so that she could interrogate them about the Thread of the Weave. Unknown to the drow, the houseservant Maisby had escaped back to Eveningstar and sought help, getting first a pair of Purple Dragons and then a team of adventurers to investigate the farmhouse. Unfortunately, Vicala Szind had set all ten Midlers and Chandlers as part of a sacrificial life-draining spell, and the rescuers would have had to stealthily rescue each family member without Vicala noticing, lest she spitefully kill them.

Ricnan was held captive in the kitchen of the house, alongside his sister Quilynn and his son Normon, guarded by several drow invaders.

## Rift Between Worlds

The Rift Between Worlds was an immense portal opened between the world of Eberron and the Demonweb Pits, which became part of Lolth's War of Everlasting Darkness in the 1480s DR.

Description

The Rift served primarily as a means of egress for the Spinner of Shadows so that she could rejoin Lolth and abscond from Eberron with a large amount of dragonshards. As a bonus (for Lolth), the portal would continue to grow on its own and eventually consume the entire world of Eberron, adding its ruins to Lolth's domain.

History

In the late 15 century DR, Lolth sought to accumulate as much power as possible and seize the role of goddess of magic from Mystra. One such scheme involved the demonic Overlord of Eberron known as the Spinner of Shadows, who languished in the Spinner's Prison in the underworld of Khyber. Using the Lords of Dust to create a cult, the Spinner had them gather up dragonshards and weaken her bindings. Though the forces of the Silver Flame arrived to stop them, the prison was weakened enough that the Spinner gained the attention of Lolth, who tore open a hole to the Demonweb Pits. Lolth beckoned her in and they departed together, leaving the Rift open and inexorably expanding. Though the Church of the Silver Flame, the Silver Flame Guardian, the Gatekeepers, and the Twelve worked to contain it, the Rift was simply too large and unstable to be stopped.

The Gatekeeper Tavnar Kronnak sent a team of bold adventurers through the Rift in the hopes of finding a way to stop it, which led to a strange and extended adventure through the Demonweb Pits (meeting Elminster along the way) and eventually into the unknown realm of Cormyr in Faerûn, which was also a victim of Lolth's malice. The adventurers helped defend against the Sschindylryn invasion of Eveningstar, and eventually returned to the Demonweb to fight Lolth in her own domain. Working with the sage Valana, they were sent back to the Rift at the very doorstep of Eberron to perform a kind of blood ritual that would attenuate its expansion enough that it stabilized. The Rift was heavily guarded by Lolth's minions, including the draegloth champion Khaszlokhar, who watched over the Rift as well as an Orb of Lolth. He fell upon the adventurers while they were weakened by their own sacrifices, but the Eberronians were still able to kill the draegloth, stop the Rift's expansion, and capture the Orb of Lolth before escaping. Though the Rift remained intact for the time being, it was no longer an existential threat to Eberron.

## Rite of Stolen Life

The Rite of Stolen Life was a rare ritual known by select worshipers of Myrkul that could transform a dead creature into a zombie-like undead version of itself that possessed the memories it had when it was alive.

Effects
The Rite involved evil magic that brought a subject back to "life" as a rotting, walking corpse. The risen individual lacked their own, original soul, and instead was animated with a substitute soul from a live sacrifice. This new soul was made to imitate the original soul by the grace of Myrkul, who imbued it with the knowledge, personality, and skills that the subject had in life. Because of this use of an alternative soul, the ritual did not require that the subject's actual soul be free or even willing for them to be reanimated, which was normally a limitation of spells like resurrection or raise dead.

Upon completion of the ritual, the dead subject awoke as if from a long slumber. As an undead creature, they had no need to breathe, eat, drink, or sleep. Their body also continued to decay as a normal corpse, and this could not be slowed or stopped. If returned to life using a conventional revival spell, the subject lost all memories of the time when they were undead.

Components
The ritual required the mostly intact corpse of the subject to be revived, a live humanoid to be sacrificed, and a large gemstone worth at least 100 gold pieces. Over the course of the one-hour ritual, the sacrifice was slain and its soul captured within the gemstone, which was then magically embedded into the forehead of the subject's corpse and imbued with the subject's memories with a prayer to Myrkul. As long as the subject's body was physically intact enough to move, the soul within the gemstone would continue to animate it. If not, the gemstone would shatter and the subject was dead once more.

History

When resurrection magic ceased to function during the effects of the death curse in the late 15 century DR, the Rite of Stolen Life was one of the only means of bringing a dead person "back". At this time, the only individual in Chult who knew the ritual was the green hag Nanny Pu'pu, and she was only willing to perform the ritual for those who did her certain favors or brought her sinister trophies. During this time, the lich Makos encountered her, and had her use the Rite to revive his comrade, Celeste, who had been captured and killed by Batiri goblins. Makos subsequently had the idea to weaponize the death curse against the liches of Thay—who were starved for souls by its effects—while using the Rite of Stolen Life to preserve important living individuals who would otherwise also succumb to the curse's effects.

## Riverside Rats

The Riverside Rats were a gang of wererats who lived in Wheloon Prison during the late 15 century DR.

History

The Rats were relatively benign as far as Wheloon gangs went, and much preferred to sneak and spy over fighting and struggling. They enjoyed a semi-sanctioned state with the Cormyrean jailkeepers, who allowed them a level of mischief in exchange for not causing problems (and significant bribery). When the Sharran Night Brigade gang fell upon them, the Rats were almost destroyed, with many slain or taken captive. They sought aid from adventurers outside the walls of the prison and helped to sneak them in via the Wererat's Entrance, giving them special talismans that let them transform into rats. This allowed their hired allies to sneak past the Night Brigade defenses and assault their leadership directly.

First the Rats committed a strike on the Battlemaster's Arenas, rescuing a number of captive wererats and slaying Agarta the Breaker. Then they struck at the Master Thief's Hideout and caught Karissa the Knife in her lair with no escape. The twisted Darstil Vurusk was assassinated in his tower, ending his mad experiments. Finally, they attacked the Temple of Shar that Storna Harnwood had built atop the ruins of their nests. By the light of Selûne, the darkness of Shar was dispelled from the temple and the priestess slain, putting an end to the gang and allowing the Riverside Rats to finally return home.

Members
Many members of the Rats were slain by the Night Brigade. The survivors included:
 Garmor the Grim
 Neese the Burrower
 Ryndalla Cuttail
 Old Tarlow
 Wyshbryn Saldar

## Rivenhelm

Rivenhelm was a knight from Port Llast on the Sword Coast North in the mid–14 century DR. Notably, he was a wielder of  Namarra, the Sword That Never Sleeps, and was considered one of the greatest knights of Faerûn in his day.

Relationships
He was the son of Stormraven, the Mistress of Port Llast, and Pardulph of Neverwinter. Stormraven granted Namarra to Rivenhelm.

History

One day, Rivenhelm was commanding a small band who were assaulted by the Flaming Fist mercenary company, and was mortally wounded. Over the course of the day, the adventurer mercenary Tamper Tencoin—a former member of the Flaming Fist—stood over Rivenhelm in a valiant effort to save his life. Finally, as night fell, the Flaming Fist stepped back and sent their tracking dogs in to savage the stricken defenders in the darkness. Then the dying Rivenhelm thrust Namarra into Tamper's hands and crawled away, shedding blood as he went. Rivenhelm strangled one dog before half a dozen mauled him to death, but Tamper got away. In time, Tamper became famed as the wielder of the Sword That Never Sleeps and was an ally of the Harpers.

## Ring of earth elemental command

A ring of earth elemental command was a ring of elemental command attuned to the Elemental Plane of Earth, giving the bearer advantages against beings from that plane, and some of those same beings' advantages.

Description
Rings of earth elemental command bore powerful dweomers and while many radiated their powerful enchantments, other rings hid their power from most types of detection, instead seeming to be rings of feather falling.

Powers
The least subtle rings gave the wearer a constant advantage against creatures from the Elemental Plane of Earth, hindered attacks from those same creatures, and let bearer understand Terran, alongside giving them the ability to move through rubble, rocks, and dirt as they were a normal road.

At the same time, the rings had five charges that could be actively used to cast dominate monster on earth elementals; these charges were restored daily at dawn. If the wearer killed an earth elemental while wearing the ring, they would gain resistance to acid attacks, the ability to move through solid rock as if it was mere difficult terrain, and the ability to cast either stone shape, stoneskin, or wall of stone by using two charges of the ring. These advantages lasted until the ring changed ownership.

The more subtle rings, however, gave the bearer an aura of  where earth elementals could not even approach, being actively repelled. The wearer could end the aura and instead attempt to charm earth elementals, but unlike the less-subtle rings, there was a real risk of the elemental resisting and attacking the wearer instead. However like all rings, other creatures from the elemental plane would be hindered in their attacks. The weearer could still speak Terran, but in addition creatures from the Elemental Plane of Earth would either be compelled to respect the wearer if they aligned, or fear them if they did not. The ring would help against petrification attempts on the wearer and grant the ability to cast stone tell once a day, passwall twice a day, wall of stone once a day, stone to flesh twice a week, move earth once a week, and featherfall as much as desired.

History
The very first ring of earth elemental command was created during Netheril's Golden Age (roughly  to ) by the preeminent elementalist Yrix Alquinnar. It was from his work that all 'lesser' rings were copied by skilled creators through the long ages.

Creation
To create a ring of earth elemental command required firstly a very experienced spellcaster, who not only specialized in forging rings, but also was able to cast summon monster VI and either stone tell, passwall, wall of stone, stone to flesh, move earth, and featherfall or dominate monster, stone shape, stoneskin, and wall of stone, depending on which type of the ring they would create. Additionally various ingredients costing around 200,000 gp would be required.

Notable Owners
 Adolmus, a human priest.
 Grazia Drodeen, a drow matron mother of House Drodeen.
 Worden Ironfist, a  dwarven fighter and adventurer.

## Ring of water elemental command

A ring of water elemental command was a ring of elemental command attuned to the Elemental Plane of Water, giving the wearer advantages against creatures from that plane, and some of those same creatures' advantages.

Description
Rings of water elemental command bore powerful dweomers and while many radiated their powerful enchantments, other rings hid their power from most types of detection, instead seeming to be rings of water walking.

Powers
The least subtle rings gave the wearer a constant advantage against creatures from the Elemental Plane of Water, hindered attacks from those same creatures, and let wearers understand Aquan, additionally it gave them the ability to move on water as if it was solid ground.

The rings had five charges that could be actively used to cast dominate monster on water elementals; these charges were restored daily at dawn. If the wearer killed a water elemental while wearing the ring, they would gain the ability to breath underwater and swim as fast as they could walk. They also got the a ability to cast either create or destroy water, control water, ice storm, or wall of ice by using two charges of the ring. These advantages lasted until the ring changed ownership.

The more subtle rings, however, gave the bearer an aura of  where water elementals could not even approach, being actively repelled. The wearer could end the aura and instead attempt to charm water elementals, but unlike the less-subtle rings, there was a real risk of the elemental resisting and attacking the wearer instead. However like all rings, other creatures from the elemental plane would be hindered in their attacks. The wearer could still speak Aquan, but in addition creatures from the Elemental Plane of Water would either be compelled to respect the wearer if they aligned, or fear them if they did not. The ring would help against fire attacks on the wearer and grant the ability to cast airy water, purify water, water breathing in a  radius, and waterwalking as often as desired, create water, and wall of ice once a day, with lower water, and part water twice a week.

History
The very first ring of water elemental command was created during Netheril's Golden Age (roughly  to ) by the preeminent elementalist Yrix Alquinnar. It was from his work that all 'lesser' rings were copied by skilled creators through the long ages.

Creation
To create a ring of water elemental command required firstly a very experienced spellcaster, who not only specialized in forging rings, but also was able to cast summon monster VI and either water walk, create water, water breathing, wall of ice, ice storm, and control weather or dominate monster, create or destroy water, control water, ice storm, and wall of ice, depending on which type of the ring they would create. Additionally various ingredients costing around 200,000 gp would be required.

## Ring of mindcloaking

A ring of mindcloaking was a magic item, similar to a ring of mind shielding, that protected the wearer from a variety of effects, no matter if they were psionic or magical in nature. Unlike the ring of mind shielding, however, a ring of mindcloaking was able to project false information in response to the probing, appearing as if the wearer's mind was unguarded.

Powers
A ring of mindcloaking protected the wearer from a greater number of phenomena than a ring of mind shielding. The psionic discipline of ESP was one such example, as were spells that allowed one to know alignment, detect evil, or even detect lies, but this was not necessarily an exhaustive list. A ring of mindcloaking wielded by a sufficiently experienced wearer could then be used to present different thoughts and an altered alignment—this not only blocked, but actively deceived, any attempts to divine the contents of their user's mind.

Notable Owners

Rings of mindcloaking were in frequent use among the more influential members of the Kraken Society, a mysterious information network that operated primarily in the Savage North and nearby Trackless Sea. Prior to his death, Alahar Khaumfros, a member of the group and Waterbaron of Yartar, was one wearer of such a ring.

Appendix
See Also
 A ring of false alignment served a similar purpose, though falsified only the wearer's alignment.
References

---

## Ring of farscrying

A ring of farscrying was a enchanted ring that allowed its wearer to contact a paired ring by way of a minuscule portal, enabling both visual and audible communication across vast distances.

Powers
The source of a ring of farscrying's portal was the large onyx gem borne on the head of the ring. At the wielder's command, it would transform into an undersized magical opening, though still one large enough to be both heard and seen through. This necessitated the activation of the portal on the matching ring—a process that could last, at most, a minute every 24 hours—so each ring of farscrying was also able to communicate a subtle tingle to its twin, signalling the other wearer.

History
Rings of farscrying were ancient devices, for at least two were found in the ruins of a Netherese enclave. Although it, like most others, had crashed from the sky in the, the trajectory of this particular Netherese city took it into the cold waves of the Trackless Sea. There, somewhere on the ocean floor among the Korinn Archipelago islands, the wreckage and the rings lay lost. They were not found until centuries later when the enclave's remains were scavenged by Slarkrethel, a kraken of great magical potential, who recovered two rings of farscrying from the ruins.

Notable Owners

Pairs of rings of farscrying were notably given to members of the Kraken Society, the widespread information network that served Slarkrethel. This was due to the fact that, upon recovery of the original rings, the kraken was able to recreate the process of their fabrication, allowing him to make many more. By way of the rings' magic, his minions were able to more readily communicate amongst each other, as well as with their mysterious leader.

Appendix
See Also
 Sending stones were a similar magic item, though they communicated purely in an audible capacity.

References

---

## Ring of the wood elves

The ring of the wood elves was a unique enchanted elegant silver band created by the wood elves of Neverwinter Wood sometime before the late 14 century DR.

Powers
The ring's only enchantment was protection against spells – it boosted the wearer's resistance to magics aimed at them.

History
The ring was crafted by the wood elves of Neverwinter Wood as the reward for young Lady Aribeth de Tylmarande, a once-revered hero who hailed from the village of Thundertree. Aribeth was instrumental in saving her hometown from a power-hungry mage from Luskan. Ansal Bloodshoulder, the informal leader of Thundertree at the time, presented the ring of the wood elves to Aribeth and the band became the hero's dearest possession. Hover, when Aribeth set out to become a paladin of Tyr, the Just God, she felt the need to break from her past. The hero thought she had to shed parts of herself to free up space for the deity's divine light and for Tyr to find her unburdened. The ring of the wood elves was one of the parts of herself the paladin chose to discard and, for one reason or another, she no longer recognized the ring as hers.

Since then, the ring of the wood elves passed several hands. One of the ring's owners was Aryvindaire, a trader and a leader of a wood elven gang that resided within the Neverwinter Wood in the late 14 century DR, sometime after the .

## Ring of the frozen falls

The ring of the frozen falls was an enchanted ring from the cold lands of Rashemen in the Unapproachable East, said to have had been created by the land's telthors.

Powers
The wearer of the ring of the frozen falls received partial immunity to cold, both natural and supernatural. They also could use the hypothermia spell thrice per day as well as polar ray once per day.

History
As it was tradition before embarking on one's dai jemma, one young man camped at the Falls of Erech, a grand waterfall on the northwestern-most edge of Rashemen. All those who set out on their journey of adulthood were to spend at least one night at the Falls of Erech and give an offering to the raging waters. So did the young man before going to sleep. As he slept, a group of Nar barbarians discovered him and began circling the young Raashemaar, intending to murder and rob him. However, before the dark deed could commence, a spirit possessed the young man, and he easily defeated the attackers, showing stunning skill and powerful savagery. Once the attackers were all dead, the spirit left the youth's body. He looked around and found a new ring on his finger. It was a protective gift from the spirit.

In the, one such ring was found witin the Bear God's Barrow north of Mulsantir.

Appearances

## Riker

The Riker family, also known as the Koart family, were a Ffolk noble family on Gwynneth in the Moonshae Isles.It is not clear if the interchangeable use of both "Riker" and "Koart" in Dragon Magazine Issue #362: "Grand History of the Realms: The Moonshaes" is intentional or the result of a mistake. As written, the two families appear to be the same.

History
Christopher Riker, also known as Christopher Koart, was a champion of High King Cymrych Hugh when he triumphed over Kazgaroth in the 3 century DR. Christopher was made the Regent of Corwell after the High King established his new capital at Caer Callidyrr, and would pass that title on to his son, Cedric Riker.

Like the royal families of the Moonshaes, the Rikers adopted the name of Cymrych as they intermarried into that family. However, following the decline of the Cymrych dynasty in the 10 century DR, the name of Riker reemerged.

By the mid–14 century DR, the cantrev of Koart was ruled by lords of the Koart family. As of the, the ruling Lord Koart also participated in the council that advised the Kendrick king at Caer Corwell.

## Rift rose

Rift rose was a type of flowering plant, a variety of rose, found growing only in the Spellplagued Chasm of Neverwinter. Its flowers had a strangely beautiful appearance.

History

In the course of their duties defending Neverwinter from the Chasm, Private Olliver of Scar Company noticed that Private Mara Taliren was still despondent over becoming heavily spellscarred. Seeking to cheer her up a bit, Olliver had someone collect the local rift roses and present them "from an admirer." Shocked and confused by the unexpected gift, Mara was still pleased by the gesture.

## Rina Belostos

Rina Belostos was a human Harper agent who worked in Eveningstar in Cormyr in the the mid-1480s DR.

Description
Rina had dark skin, long black hair, and blue eyes. She wore an unassuming brown outfit and carried a lute for her cover as a musician.

History

Around the time of the Shadovar invasion of Wheloon, Rina Belostos led a Harper cell in Cormyr. When Oriphaun Huntsilver stole the Abyssal Planus Mechanus Nether Scroll from the shade lord Tyvrus in Wheloon Prison, she briefly met with Oriphaun and recruited him to the cause—only for him to vanish the next day with the Nether Scroll while pursued by the agents of Returned Netheril. She led her group of Harpers to Eveningstar to look for the Nether Scroll and Oriphaun, finding his journal that described his plan to tear the Scroll to pieces and hide them along the westward High Road that led to the Storm Horns. She and her team posed as traveling musicians while in Eveningstar, gathering information from the local Evenors, Antone Corluk and Belvedere of House Huntsilver, and even Elminster. She was joined in Eveningstar by Harpers including Eradin Krall, Olyn Araphyr, the lutist Tibalt Dormen, the flutist Kordo Sweetspire, and the drummers Freda Gambosi, Golo Manks, and Vindavere Dyrwool. Unfortunately, due to the ongoing Sschindylryn invasion of Eveningstar the Purple Dragons and War Wizards were unable to assist, and the High Road was overrun with bandits, monsters, and the forces of Thultanthar.

The first piece of the Scroll was found by the wizard Harper Bensen Virandson, who was sent aid by Tibalt Dormen as they transported it down Greenweald Lane to the Harper Lodge hidden in the King's Forest. Along the way they fought Shadovar agents and mercenaries led by the fearsome Dom, receiving unexpected help from the tricky bard Nil.

The next fragment was found by Freda Gambosi at the ruined Beckside Castle, used as a rest stop along the road. Following an investigation they discovered an infiltrating Shadovar force led by Keldan Shadeskein and Gaston Wyrst, who attempted to seize the fragment but were slain in battle.

Eradin Krall located the another bit at Lost Souls' Swamp, where Oriphaun had trusted it with the hermit Seamus. Unfortunately, the medusa Sshaastess detected the magical power of the artifact and invaded the swamp, taking the Scroll fragment for herself until it was taken back by force.

The agent Kordo Sweetspire found another at the Old Man's Face inn at Tyrluk, where Oriphaun had ingeniously hidden it in plain sight. Following a brief battle with the forces of Tarys and Wyle at the inn, the Scroll was found and recovered.

Disaster struck when Olyn Araphyr sent reinforcements to meet Girmond Dyle, who had taken custody of the collected pieces. The Netherese found and killed Girmond first, taking his Scroll pieces and assembling the whole Nether Scroll at Granwell Keep. Worse still, the Netherese accidentally caused a portal to the Abyss to open, inviting the glabrezu Razagnol to perform a minor planar invasion. After a three-way battle between Shadovar, demons, and Harpers, the day was won by the forces of good. Rina arrived a moment late with Elminster via teleportation, apologizing for the tardiness, and had Elminster take the Nether Scroll away for safekeeping, lest some monster or evil empire take it.

## Ritual of the Waxing

The Ritual of the Waxing was a ritual of the Church of Selûne.

Description
The Ritual of the Waxing could only be performed by a follower of Selûne on the night after the new moon.

History
In the, the goddess Selûne, the Moonmaiden, may have sent Kyriani Agrivar of the Selûne's Smile of Waterdeep a vision, upon which Kyri would recruit a band of adventurers friendly to the church for a quest. She would bid them take the Eye of Selûne artifact to Elah'zad, an oasis in the Sword of Anauroch called the House of the Moon, and perform the Ritual of the Waxing at the center of the Selûnite temple there. This would open a temporary portal to the Gates of the Moon and call back to Toril the ancient Netherese floating city of Selûnnara, which would wage war against the Shadovar of Thultanthar, the rival floating city that lay nearby. On the way, they would be opposed by Shadover and Sharrans. This battle was part of the eternal conflict between Selûne and Shar.

## Richard Hamblen

Richard Hamblen is a board game designer whose contribution to the Forgotten Realms is co-designing the board game The Great Khan Game.

History
Hamblen was born on February 2, 1945, in Albuquerque, New Mexico. In 1975, he became a part of Avalon Hill's editorial staff. His first two board games were both released in 1977: Victory in the Pacific and Victory at Sea. He won the 1977 Charles S. Roberts Best Strategic Game award for Victory in the Pacific.

His sole known contribution to the Forgotten Realms or Dungeons & Dragons is co-designing The Great Khan Game with Tom Wham.

Works

Board Games
 Victory in the Pacific (1977)
 Victory at Sea (1977)
 The Arab-Israeli Wars: Tank Battles in the Mideast (1977)
 Fortress Europa (1978)
 Magic Realms (1979)
 Gunslinger (1982)
 Bull Run: The First Major Battle of the American Civil War (1983)
 Merchant of Venus (1988)
 The Great Khan Game (1989)
 Merchant of Venus – Second Edition (2012)

Board Game Expansions
 Merchant of Venus: The Variant Mini Expansion (1990)

## Ring of the spellflinger

Rings of the spellflinger were a variety of enchanted rings from the Chultan city of Port Nyanzaru in the late 15 century DR.

Powers
Rings of the spellflinger were created with enchantments of various power; however, all of them boosted the wearer's ability to deal well-aimed, devastating magical attacks and boosted their arcane recoveries. Lastly, these rings increased the strength of offensive spells and magical abilities when unleashed from at least  away from the target.

History
In the late 15 century DR, as the death curse ravaged Faerûn, the famed author Volothamp Geddarm found himself in the Hunter's Square in Port Nyanzaru, a city in Chult. As he was working on Volo's Guide to Monsters, Volo enlisted the aid of adventurers from the North to hunt down and slay a collection of powerful beasts of legend and report to him with their experiences and loot. A number of these rings were offered as rewards for various marks, such as the Tyrant, Kutlass, and Erjellel.

## Richemulot

Richemulot was one of the Domains of Dread. It was a wilderness where cities were half-empty, and information was power.

Description
The domain of Richemulot was largely wilderness, with sparse holdings; its towns had elaborate sewers, often infested by and at least a third of the buildings were empty and dilapidated. Its people did not cling to material wealth, and all things were seen as ultimately hand-me-downs, even land. Many people were known to vanish without a trace, to the point this did not draw much attention.

## Rina the Keen-Blade

Rina the Keen-Blade was a Harper agent and weapons expert who worked in Eveningstar in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Rina had dark skin and long brown hair worn in a ponytail. She wore a suit of fine chain mail.

History

Around the time of the Sschindylryn invasion of Eveningstar during the 1480s DR, Rina joined Kara Patwin and Lorant Tass in following up on Oriphaun Huntsilver's search of the Storm Horns, where he found Shadovar activity. When the orcs working for Amskar launched a preemptive and failed raid upon Eveningstar, Rina and the hunter Crowler Johns examined the odd armaments the orcs had carried. She quickly came to the conclusion that they were being supplied by Risen Netheril, and put that together with reports from the scout Kyra Armond, who spotted suspicious movement at Tumblerock Pass. Rina dispatched a team to join Kyra there and assault the Netherese caravans, defeating General Grablandurr and ruining their efforts to bring weapons into Cormyr.

## River Gruus

The River Gruus was a river that flowed from the westernmostAlthough the map included with The Great Khan Game features a cryptic compass rose, Crown of Salt states that Agenais is on the southernmost island of the Whamite Isles. The orientation of the map can be extrapolated from there. of the Whamite Isles into the Sea of Fallen Stars. The river was a key location in a series of wild conflicts between various nations and pirates of the Whamite Isles at some point prior to the mass death of the population in the .

The River Gruus had its source in the mountains that seperated Veldergauttland from the rest of the island. It flowed down along the isle, forming a border between Bolgor and Penbroc. It then turned sharply away from Bolgor, and formed Penbroc's border with Graubünden, then emptied into the Sea of Fallen Stars. The historic fortress Gruuscastle overlooked the river, and was its most significant landmark. It was built on the spot where the borders of Graubünden, Penbroc and Bolgor met.

The Gruusboatmen of Bolgor were known to row up and down the river's waters.
